### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 歐洲企業協會(Business Europe)發布一項調查顯示，自 6月以來，歐洲企業對工
業和服務業前景的信心有所改善，惟對通膨上升和主權債務問題的擔憂日益加
劇。由於美伊戰爭推高能源成本，而歐盟絕大部分能源依賴進口，能源價格上漲
進一步傳導至零售價格，導致今年通膨升至歐洲央行 2%目標水準之上，市場預
期歐洲央行將於 12月再度升息。
 美國舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴利(Mary Daly)表示，價格壓力正從能源和商品擴散
至服務業，而服務業通膨可能非常頑固。潛在通膨可能並未像市場預期的降
溫。近期能源價格回落，拉低整體通膨，然而核心通膨仍持續往上，聯準會需
要進一步升息以應對居高不下的通膨。此外，聯準會理事克里斯沃勒
(Christopher Waller)及達拉斯聯準銀行總裁洛根(Lorie Logan)均提出升息兩碼的
可能性，多位聯準會官員頻頻釋放偏鷹言論，升息預期升溫，預計將支撐美元

走勢。
 日本 10 月企業商品物價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝
突及 AI相關需求推升能源和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，
有利日幣走升。
 美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%雙雙高於預期，10月 PPI年
增率亦升至 6.5%，為四年來新高，通膨居高不下，FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機
率達九成，2027年 1 月再升息一碼機率逾六成。
 台灣 10月出口 967.6 億美元，為史上單月首度突破 900億美元，年增率 58.9%
為連續 36個月正成長，創下史上最長成長周期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.794，低點在 32.994，以 32.838 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 7 分。 
    上週台幣走貶，週初，台股反彈，台幣一度走升至 32.794週內高點，不過，外
資延續匯出走勢，台幣隨即收斂所有漲幅，並貶回 32.950 上方；週間，美國公布 10
月 CPI數據表現優於預期，數據強化 12月升息一碼的預期，同時也開始反應升息兩
碼的風險，美元指數強勢突破 103 整數關卡，美元利差優勢擴大，台幣承壓走低至
32.994週內低點；臨近週末，台股走揚，外資轉為買超台股並匯入，台幣升破 32.830，
隨後匯價逢低吸引進口商買盤進場，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.838。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，顯
示通膨壓力仍在，市場預期聯準會 12月升息一碼，也不排除一口氣升息兩碼，美元
利差優勢將不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來看，上週公佈台灣 10月出口 967.6億美元，
是史上單月首度突破 900 億美元，寫下單月新高，且年增率 58.9%創下連續 36紅的
表現。基本面強勁，將有助於台幣的表現；盤勢上來看，輝達股價下跌風波，連帶
影響台股表現，整體外資仍偏向匯出，台幣仍面臨貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本
週台幣走勢偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.600-33.200

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。週初，川普表示若伊朗願意簽署具有實質內容的協議，
美方就不太可能重新展開大規模的攻擊，帶動市場進一步降低戰事升溫的預期，油
價下跌，風險偏好回升，減弱美元避險需求，人民幣受益走升至 6.6650；週間，聯
準會官員沃勒鷹派發言，加以美國 10月 CPI表現優於預期，帶動美債殖利率及美元
指數走揚人民幣走貶至 6.6800；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%，創下 2022
年 11 月以來高點，顯示通膨壓力難以消退，美元指數高檔震盪，人民幣於 6.6700上
方走勢偏弱，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6746，週線貶值 10點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6726，
週線升值 37點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，
強化市場對於聯準會 12月升息的預期，利差因素支撐美元指數反彈，將抑制人民幣
走升；就中國國內市場來看，中國最新公布 10 月貿易順差規模持續超過 1000 億美
元，出口商結匯需求將緩解人民幣貶值壓力；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖
中，USD/CNH重返五日均線之上，顯示人民幣強勢升值的趨勢暫歇，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走弱。週初，USD/JPY 在 158 整數關卡附近波動；週間，美國 10 月
CPI數據表現優於預期，且多位聯準會官員發表鷹派言論，帶動美元指數回升至 102
以上，USD/JPY 上行突破 160 整數關卡；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%為
四年來新高，USD/JPY持穩於高位，終場收在 159.66，週線上漲 121 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本公布 10 月企業商品物
價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝突及 AI相關需求推升能源
和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，預計將支撐日幣走勢。二、
美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%皆高於預期，10 月 PPI 亦升至高
位，加之多位聯準會官員鷹派發言，FEDWATCH顯示 12月升息機率已上升至 90%，
明年 1 月再升息一碼機率也超過 60%，美元持穩於 102以上高位，將有利 USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月表示近期匯市走勢出現單邊快速波動，日本政府
將繼續與美國財政部密切合作，並採取適當應對措施，日本政府逢高採取干預將限
縮 USD/JPY上行空間。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY突破七周來的主
要交易區間以及年線壓力，在 160 整數關口徘徊，短線多方動能較佔優勢，然而匯
價來至高點有日本政府干預風險，預期將限縮上行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：158.50-161.00 
前週交易區間 
157.65 160.18

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，市場對於美伊衝突的擔憂有所緩解，風險偏
好回升，國際油價及美元指數同步下行，EUR/USD 自上周低點 1.1135 附近走升最
高來至 1.1250；週間，美國公布 10月份 CPI顯示通膨仍處高位且多位聯準會官員鷹
派談話，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.11 整數關卡；臨近週末，美國 10 月份 PPI 年增率
6.5%為四年來新高，EUR/USD續跌，終場收在 1.1021，週線下跌 122 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、德國 10 月 CPI 月減 0.2%
年增 2.8%，與預期相符且較 9 月下滑，顯示通膨有所放緩；此外德國央行總裁暨歐
洲央行管委納格爾表示德國經濟可能在第四季加速，儘管能源價格在美國期中選舉
後下跌，但因通膨仍高於 2%的目標，下個月升息依然是顯而易見，歐洲央行維持鷹
派態度將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 10月 CPI及 PPI 顯示通膨依然頑固且有向更廣泛
經濟領域擴散的趨勢，此外多位聯準會官員發表談話，其中理事沃勒及達拉斯聯儲
總裁洛根均提出升息 2 碼的可能性，升息預期增加將持續支撐美元。三、技術面而
言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，近期匯價接連跌破 1.12 及 1.11
整數關卡，短線空方動能強勁，短線上以震盪偏弱看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.0900-1.1150 
前週交易區間 
1.1019 1.1251

### Page 7

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.11.09-115.11.13 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109/A15109R 1.86%~1.93% 
－ 5年期- A15108 1.71%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週初央行積極減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，雖台股連
日回升，不過資金需求未明顯增加，銀行間交投較為清淡，帶動短率靠向區間
低位。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.542%，低於市
場預期區間 1.56%-1.57%，較上次標售結果下跌 3.8 個基本點。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.820%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.67%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1個基本點。 
二、 本週台債殖利率先下後上，週初先落後反應上週美債利率下跌走勢，5 年券
A15108、10年券 A15109分別向下成交在 1.71%、1.86%，後因美債利率反彈，
10 年券 A15109R週五成交在 1.93%。 
受美國 CPI 高於預期、多位官員表達偏鷹派言論、川普表示無須與伊朗達成
協議影響，美債利率回升，加上台灣央行年底升息可能性尚未排除，買盤缺乏
追價動力，預期短線台債利率呈區間盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，
10 年券區間為 1.90%~2.00%。

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷

市場焦點
美國
• 10月零售銷售月增0.2%，整體增幅略緩，但與GDP高度相關的控制組零售銷售月增0.6%，顯
示高利率環境下消費需求仍具韌性，內需仍是支撐美國經濟的重要力量，然而可支配所得成
長趨緩、儲蓄率維持低檔，且家庭支出連續多月高於薪資成長，反映部分消費來自信用擴張
而非所得改善。整體而言，美國消費短期仍支撐經濟，但家庭購買力壓力正逐漸積累，未來
內需續航力仍有待觀察。
中國
• H200晶片的出口管制反覆政策變化，促使中國企業加速開發本土替代產品，加上中國經濟正
從房地產開發，轉向高科技帶動經濟成長，彭博預估2026年中國高科技產業的規模將達到27
兆元人民幣，占GDP的18.3%。總結來說，美中科技戰將使全球AI 供應鏈逐漸形成兩套體系，
以美國為核心，另一套以中國自主晶片為核心，長期而言這將改變半導體、AI 伺服器及資料中

心產業的競爭格局。
AI
資本支出
• 半導體研究機構Semi Analysis下修未來數年全球AI加速器出貨、資本支出、HBM需求及電
力需求預測，反映AI產業正由GPU主導的高速擴張階段，逐步轉向多元運算架構與成本效率
競爭，預估輝達受到的衝擊相對較大；博通、AMD，以及Google TPU、Amazon Trainium
等平台，則因客製化晶片與成本效益優勢，展現相對韌性。這並不代表ASIC將全面取代GPU，
而是AI算力市場的供應來源將更加多元化。

消費韌性延續、購買力承壓，美國內需強勁背後隱憂浮現
• 整體零售增幅放緩，核心消費仍具韌性：美國10月零售銷售月增0.2%，低於9月的0.8%，但與市場預期相符；與GDP
消費支出較相關的控制組零售銷售月增0.6%，優於預估0.4%，且9月數據上修，顯示核心消費仍具支撐力，非店面零售、
建材及醫療保健用品銷售維持成長，反映美國內需尚未明顯轉弱，仍是支撐經濟成長的重要動能。
• 實質所得成長受限，家庭消費續航力面臨考驗：零售銷售成長仍須留意物價因素，10月名目銷售增幅未必代表實質消費
同步擴張；9月實質可支配所得月減0.3%，儲蓄率降至3.8%，顯示家庭購買力與儲蓄緩衝能力承壓，儘管低收入家庭近
期支出仍具韌性，但通膨侵蝕實質所得，加上高利率環境持續，後續消費動能仍面臨考驗。

• 後市觀察：美國消費短期仍具韌性，但物價與融資成本偏高，可能逐步壓抑非必要性支出；建議股票優先布局具品牌優
勢、定價能力及獲利穩定的民生必需品與醫療保健類股，債券則以短天期美債及高信評債券為主，降低升息環境下的價
格波動風險。

美國通膨黏著依舊，Fed緊縮政策提高升息預期
• 通膨來源輪動，核心PCE動能再度升溫：彭博經濟研究預估10月核心PCE月增0.29%、年增3.2%，三個月年化增幅由
2.9%升至3.5%。儘管AI相關硬體、軟體及機票價格回落，有助抵銷部分通膨壓力，但處方藥、餐飲服務、地面運輸及部
分商品價格接續上漲，使核心通膨維持高檔，顯示通膨壓力持續輪動，尚未有效消退。
• 通膨黏著性升高，市場轉向升息定價：過去一年漲幅逾3%的PCE分項占比預估由49%升至51%，反映通膨改善仍不穩固；
儘管6個月年化口徑略降至45%，然短期通膨動能仍有回升跡象，加上10月PPI年增率升至6.5%，以及近期Fed官員釋出
進一步緊縮訊號，市場已提高12月升息及2027年初持續升息的預期，利率維持高檔的時間可能拉長。

• 市場看法：在通膨黏著性與升息預期同步升高下，美債殖利率及美元可能維持相對強勢，長天期債券與高評價成長股面
臨較大壓力；債券建議優先配置短天期美債、浮動利率債及高信評投資等級債，股票則以獲利穩健、現金流充足及具定
價能力的企業為核心，藉以降低因市場波動而衍生的風險。
10月核心PCE動能料反彈
資料來源：BEA, BLS, Bloomberg Economics，2026.11.17。
華許關注的PCE通膨指標10月可能未現改善

升息預期升溫、緊縮效應浮現，美債布局契機逐步醞釀
• 升息預期升溫，殖利率走高提升債券收益吸引力：Fed官員近
期密集釋出偏鷹訊號，10月FOMC會議紀錄亦顯示，多數官員
認為若通膨未能有效降溫，仍有必要進一步升息，部分官員甚
至認為不排除採取更大幅度的升息措施，市場因此提高對12月
升息的預期，10年期美債殖利率於11月中旬升至5.1%左右，
反映市場對利率維持高檔及進一步緊縮的預期；惟從債券投資
角度來看，殖利率走高也同步提升新投入資金的收益水準，若
後續通膨壓力逐步緩解，將有機會為債券價格回升創造條件。
• 緊縮政策壓抑需求，經濟降溫有望帶動債市轉機：美國經濟目
前仍受AI相關資本支出及企業投資支撐，但高利率環境將逐步
提高企業融資成本、壓抑消費與投資動能，若經濟成長因此放

緩，並帶動通膨壓力降溫，市場對未來政策利率的預期便可能
由升息轉向持平甚至下修，進而推動美債殖利率回落、債券價
格回升。尤其當經濟降溫疑慮升高時，美國公債的避險需求亦
可能增加，為短中天期債券帶來布局機會。
• 市場看法：後續應持續關注12月FOMC會議決策、通膨及就業
數據，以及升息對企業融資與民間需求的影響。建議採取穩健
防禦、分批布局策略，優先留意短中天期美國公債及投資等級
債，在殖利率相對高檔時掌握較具吸引力的收益水準；待通膨
降溫、實質殖利率回落及降息預期逐步形成，再視市場變化適
度調整存續期間，以兼顧債息收益與後續資本利得機會。

中美科技脫鉤不可能逆轉
美中科技戰升級，中國半導體自主化進程加速
• 美中科技戰升級，中國半導體自主化進程加速：輝達原本向中國銷售H200晶片，由黃仁勳與川普協商促成，根據該協
議，晶片必須經由美國運送，讓美國政府可以收取25%的關稅，近日美國公布收緊對中國AI晶片出口限制，輝達也停止
向中國銷售H200晶片，顯示美中科技戰進一步升級，與此同時，中國其實早已積極推動半導體自主化，降低對美國技
術的依賴，隨著美國封堵海外資料中心提供中國AI算力的管道，中國勢必加速採用國產替代方案，華為有望成為最大受
益者，華為昇騰系列AI處理器是輝達硬體的替代選擇。
• 展望後市：H200晶片的出口管制反覆政策變化，促使中國企業加速開發本土替代產品，加上中國經濟正從房地產開發，
轉向高科技帶動經濟成長，彭博預估2026年中國高科技產業的規模將達到27兆元人民幣，占GDP的18.3%。總結來說，

美中科技戰將使全球AI 供應鏈逐漸形成兩套體系，以美國為核心，另一套以中國自主晶片為核心，長期而言這將改變
半導體、AI 伺服器及資料中心產業的競爭格局。
黃仁勳時代中國市場對輝達銷售佔比

地產銷售與開工增速仍處低位
商品房銷售面積 房屋新開工面積 房屋竣工面積
房地產銷售和開工偏弱，開發投資承壓
• 房地產銷售和開工偏弱，開發投資承壓：2026年前三季中國房地產市場仍處於調整階段，整體復甦力道不足，1至9月房
地產開發投資年減20.6%，新建商品房銷售面積年減13.2%，顯示建商投資與購屋需求依然疲弱，雖然部分核心城市房市
出現改善跡象，但尚未有效帶動全國市場回溫，預估第四季局部市場的好轉，對全國開發投資的提振作用有限，從交易
結構來看，新房與二手房市場持續分化，9月新房銷售面積降幅較前期擴大，而二手房成交面積則年增10.7%，顯示購屋
需求轉為價格較具優勢的二手市場，價格方面，一線城市二手住宅價格9月較上月上漲，新房與二手房價格維持正成長，
但漲幅逐漸收斂，但是二、三線城市房價仍呈波動整理，尚未形成明顯回升趨勢。

• 展望後市：中國房地產開發投資累計年增率降幅，可望較2026年進一步收斂，房市銷售回暖不代表投資立即回升，當成
交改善後，房企往往優先將資金用於提升現金流、推進在建工程及履行交屋責任，以降低財務壓力並穩定營運，唯有當
住宅去化速度持續，因此市場預估2027年房地產市場將延續築底，整體呈現「銷售先穩、投資後升」的溫和修復格局。
資料來源:開源證券,2026.11.16

Semi Analysis下調AI算力資本開支
• Semi Analysis下修AI資本支出預測：半導體研究機構Semi Analysis於11月13日更新AI產業模型，下修未來數年全球AI加
速器出貨、資本支出、HBM需求及電力需求預測，其中2028至2030年調整幅度最為明顯。全球AI加速器資本支出預測分
別下調28%、41%及40%，2030年預測值由原先的1.531兆美元降至9,230億美元。
• AI收入增速放緩，融資環境成為主要壓力：此次下修主要反映AI商業化進展與資金供給不如預期。Semi Analysis指出，前
沿AI實驗室第三季新增年度經常性收入（ARR）增速放緩，使市場重新評估未來Token收入能否持續支撐龐大的算力成本；
另一方面，債權與股權投資者對AI基礎設施的融資意願降低，尤其缺乏大型雲端服務商長期採購承諾的專案，面臨較高的

建設與資金風險。此外，美國中期選舉後，資料中心監管及審批不確定性增加，預計部分原訂2027年下半年至2028年上
半年投產的資料中心將延後。不過，Semi Analysis認為，AI收入成長放緩與融資環境收緊，才是本次預測下修的主要原因。

硬體需求同步下修，供應鏈回歸正常化
• 硬體需求同步下修，供應鏈回歸正常化：除資本支出外，Semi Analysis亦下調AI硬體需求預測，2026至2030年全球AI加
速器邏輯晶片出貨面積累計下修17%；HBM方面，2028年出貨量預測由9.0EB降至6.4EB，下修30%，反映供需逐步改善
及價格漲勢趨緩。此外，2030年AI加速器功耗需求預測由122.8GW降至80.7GW，下修34%。整體而言，AI硬體需求仍
持續成長，但先前由晶片短缺、記憶體漲價及高額資本支出推動的擴張模式，正逐步回歸較合理的供需水準。
• AI產業進入效率競爭，資本支出仍具成長空間：整體而言，Semi Analysis此次下修並不代表AI需求消失，而是市場開始
重新檢視AI投資的商業回報、融資可持續性及資料中心建置效率。未來AI產業發展將不再單純追求算力規模擴張，而是更

重視運算成本、能源效率與投資報酬率。儘管AI基礎設施資本支出仍將維持成長，但成長速度可能低於先前預期，且資金
將更集中於具備明確客戶需求、穩定融資來源及較佳成本效益的建置計畫。

AI晶片供應商：輝達面臨最大下修壓力
公司 Semi Analysis 主要看法 影響與評估
輝達 未來出貨量預測遭大幅下修；大型AI實驗室轉向其他運算平台 加上Neocloud客戶融資風險提高，新一代晶片亦面臨降價壓力 本次下修幅度最大 未來營收與毛利率可能同時承壓
博通 2026至2030年ASIC業務累計營收預測下修11% 主要反映HBM成本下降帶動售價調整，而非大幅削減出貨量 相對具韌性 受惠Google TPU及大型AI客戶自研ASIC需求，性能成本比與能效具優勢
AMD 2028至2030年出貨及資本支出預測遭下修 受惠Meta、OpenAI及Anthropic等大型客戶布局，部分新產品部署時程提前 相較輝達具一定韌性 但中長期仍面臨AI需求及資本支出下修風險

雲端平台與AI模型業者：自研晶片及運算成本成為關鍵
公司 Semi Analysis 主要看法 影響與評估
亞馬遜 Trainium在2027及2028年的出貨預測未遭下修，主因資料中心專案儲備充足、 晶片製造成本較低，且願意透過策略性補貼支持重要客戶 少數維持近端成長預期的平台，自研ASIC有助降低對外部GPU的依賴
Google TPU具備性能成本比及能源效率優勢 是大型AI實驗室採購的重要替代方案 自研ASIC有助降低運算成本，提升AI基礎設施競爭力
OpenAI 除持續使用GPU，也將AMD GPU及自研ASIC納入運算平台布局 算力來源逐步多元化，有助降低對單一GPU供應商的依賴
Anthropic 採用TPU、Trainium、AMD GPU及輝達GPU等四大運算平台；從性能成本 比考量，輝達GPU相對處於較不具優勢的位置 有利於具備成本及能效優勢的ASIC，替代GPU平台
Meta 與AMD透過認股權證安排建立合作關係，並布局客製化AI晶片 有助AMD取得大型客戶訂單，大型科技公司積極推動算力來源多元化

Semi Analysis：成長放緩但未停止
成長放緩，而非停止
2030年資本支出仍達9,230億美元，高於2026年的3,920億美元。
競爭焦點轉移
從單純性能比拼，轉向性能成本比、能效與融資能力的綜合較量。

3 輝達壓力大於部分ASIC供應商
地位仍強，但成長面臨更大壓力；AMD、博通與亞馬遜Trainium相對穩健。
觀察面向 SemiAnalysis釋出的訊號
GPU市場 輝達仍具市場領先地位，但大型客戶開始重視成本效益，降低對單一GPU平台的依賴。
ASIC市場 Google TPU、Amazon Trainium及客製化ASIC逐漸取得競爭優勢。
AI資本支出 AI收入成長放緩及融資環境收緊，使資料中心投資計畫面臨重新評估。
Neocloud 缺乏大型雲端業者長期採購承諾的業者，融資與專案執行風險較高。

產業競爭 未來競爭核心將由單純追求算力規模，轉向性能成本比、能源效率及融資能力。

經理人經濟軟著陸成主流共識
• 根據11月份美銀美林經理人調查報告顯示經濟軟著陸成主流共識，過去引領全球股市狂飆的人工智慧(AI)題材正進
入壓力測試期，資金開始從高度集中的科技股撤出，轉向具備防禦性與合理估值的板塊。
• AI資本支出受檢視，科技股減碼創20年之最：過去市場無條件追捧AI概念股的風氣已經改變，法人機構開始用放大
鏡檢視企業的變現能力與財務體質，AI投資從擴張轉向檢視，高達淨38%的經理人認為目前企業在AI領域投資過度，
創下調查歷史新高；更有52%經理人預期未來半年內將有超大規模雲端服務商宣布削減資本支出。同時，更希望企
業將現金流用於改善資產負債表(42%)或增加股東回饋(33%)，僅16%支持繼續擴大資本支出。
• 尾端風險和最擁擠交易：AI泡沫(34%)與AI超大規模雲端服務商信用事件(31%)包辦市場前兩大尾端風險；此外，

38%經理人擔憂大型雲端商發行的債券可能成為系統性信用風險來源。作多全球半導體雖仍是最擁擠交易，但認同
度已從7月高峰的82%大幅滑落至39%，撤出的資金明顯轉向防禦型的醫療類股(淨加碼升至34%，躍居最受青睞產
業)、債券(減碼幅度由淨37%收斂至淨23%)以及消費類股。展望2027年，經理人最看好的資產類別依序為國際股票
(36%)、美股(24%)與黃金(17%)。
• 小結及建議：整體而言，在經濟溫和軟著陸背景下，投資策略建議多元配置、股債兼備以抵抗波動。
資料來源：美銀美林證券，富蘭克林投顧整理，2026.11

付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

## Document 078: 2026年7-9月期GDP予測(最終版) ～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026 年 11 月10 日

1/2 
2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測(最終版) 
～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

11 月16 日に公表される26 年７-９月期の実質
ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率▲0.4％（前期比▲
0.1％）と予測する。10 月 30 日の段階では前期
比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋0.1％）と小幅プラス
成長を予想していたが、その後公表された経済
指標の結果を反映し、小幅マイナス成長へと予
測値を下方修正する。 
本日公表された26 年９月分の国際収支統計の
結果を反映し、26 年７-９月期の実質輸出を前期
比＋0.1％（従来予測値：同＋1.2％）へ下方修
正する。筆者の９月分の想定と比べてサービス
輸出が大きく下振れたことが主因である。実質
輸入も前期比＋3.0％（従来予測値：同＋
3.3％）と下方修正するが、輸出の下方修正度合
いの方が大きいことから、外需の予測値を前期
比年率寄与度▲1.9％Pt（従来予測値：同▲
1.3％Pt）へ下方修正している。 
また、９月分の家計調査と家計消費状況調査
が想定よりも下振れたことを反映し、個人消費
の予測値も前期比▲0.3％と、従来予測値の同▲
0.2％から小幅下方修正した。

物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
ことが成長率を押し下げる見込みである。特に、８～９月には長雨・大雨や台風などの天候不順
が相次ぎ、外出手控えや旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて消費が下押しされたとみられる。一
方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えするとみられる。設備投資の伸びには特殊要因も
（％）
実質ＧＤＰ ▲ 0.1

2/2
一部含まれているが、それを除いても堅調だったと考えられる。
７-９月期は４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になると予想するが、マイナス幅は大きくない。今回
のマイナス成長には天候不順という一時的な下押し要因も含まれており、過去３四半期に高い成
長が続いた後であることや、企業部門の底堅さが維持されていることを踏まえれば、景気回復の
流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろう。
物価高による節約志向は根強く、個人消費の回復にはなお時間を要するとみられるが、設備投
資を中心に企業部門が好調さを維持している点は好材料である。家計部門が力強さを欠く一方、
企業部門が景気を下支えする構図のもと、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると判
断している。

（需要項目ごとの解説は、2026 年 7-9 月期 GDP 予測（1 次速報） ～前期比年率＋0.4％と予想。成長率は鈍化も、景気回復の流れは
崩れず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所（10 月 30 日発行）をご参照ください）

一. 3Q26 毛利率 4.75%，EPS 7.08 元 
廣達 3Q26 營收 1 兆 1155 億，QoQ +7.6% ，YoY +125.2% ，產
品組合部分，受惠於 AI 機櫃 ASP 持續走高，第三季 AI 伺服器營
收呈現季增，但出貨量則較上季略減，主要由於第三季處於新舊
產品世代的轉換期。通用伺服器受惠於 AI Agent 與資料中心需求
擴大，第三季通用型伺服器出貨量表現優於預期。而筆電部分，
第三季出貨量為 960 萬台，QoQ -17%，YoY -24%，主要由於記
憶體漲價影響，以至於今年下半年終端市場需求明顯疲弱。獲利
方面，3Q26 營業毛利 530.2 億，QoQ +1.8%，YoY +56.3%，毛
利率 4.75%，QoQ -0.27ppts，YoY -2.1ppts，公司表示，毛利率
下滑主因高單價 AI 機櫃出貨 ASP 大幅躍升產生稀釋效應，加
上筆電材料成本上揚與規模縮減所致，並非本業獲利能力惡化。
營業利益 322 億，QoQ -6.0% ，YoY +75.3% ，營利率 2.89%，
QoQ -0.41ppts ，YoY -0.82ppts 。3Q26 業外淨利 26.8 億，稅後
284.2 億，QoQ -0.8%，YoY +73.0%，單季稅後 EPS 7.08 元。

二. 4Q26 AI 伺服器動能延續，筆電持續面臨逆風 
公司公告 10 月營收 3532.5 億，MoM +8.6%，YoY +104%，今年
累計營收 3.31 兆，YoY +99.9%。展望 4Q26，公司表示，隨著新
世代 GPU 平台 AI 機櫃於第四季逐步進入小批量量產，受惠於新
產品 ASP 顯著拉升，第四季 AI 伺服器營收仍將維持強勁季增。
通用型伺服器第四季出貨量亦預計持續成長，而筆電第四季出貨
量則預期呈雙位數季減。另外，毛利率部分，由於高單價 AI 機櫃
出貨佔比提升持續稀釋毛利率，再加上筆電出貨規模縮減，預期
4Q26 毛利率將較第三季下滑。預估 4Q26 營收 1.15 兆，QoQ

於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027

貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。

高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

四. 投資建議為「買進」，目標價 385 元 
受惠於 AI 伺服器的強勁需求，公司預估明年 AI 伺服器營收將雙
位數成長，且隨美系四大 CSP 資本支出展望維持正向，GPU、ASIC
伺服器專案持續放量，將有望挹注公司營運持續增長。以公司 2027
年 EPS 來看，目前股價交易於 9~10 倍本益比，評價仍具上修空
間，給予買進投資建議，目標價為 385 元(12x 2027 EPS)。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
廣達電腦(Quanta)為全球筆記型電腦專業研發設計製造與雲端運
算解決方案領導供應商，為全球前五大筆記型電腦代工廠商。公
司主要替品牌業者從事代工服務，代工項目包含筆記型電腦、平
板電腦、智慧型手機、伺服器、商用及家用智慧物聯網裝置、消
費性電子產品、車用電子等。公司總部位於桃園龜山，生產基地
包含桃園、大陸上海、重慶、江蘇、美國田納西州、加州、墨西

二.產業趨勢概況 
1. 明年 CSP Capex 預估破 1.5 兆美元，AI 伺服器產值年增逾 9 成 
根據集邦科技(TrendForce)最新研究，受惠於 Google、Amazon、
Meta、Microsoft、Oracle 及中國四大科技巨頭等全球前九大雲端
服務供應商(CSP)擴大投資 AI 基礎設施，預估 2026 年九大 CSP
資本支出總額將逾 9,570 億美元，YoY +106%，2027 年更因伺服
器整機漲價、零組件缺貨漲價及供應商要求預付款等因素，有望
突破 1.5 兆美元，YoY +60%，雙雙優於預期。在超大型 CSP、Tier-2
資料中心與主權 AI 等需求帶動下，預估 2026 年 AI 伺服器產值約
4,790 億美元，占整體伺服器產值近 75%；2027 年隨輝達 GB300、
VR200 與超微 Helios 等整櫃式方案成為主流，加上記憶體等關鍵
零組件短缺推升單價，預計整體伺服器產值將首度跨越 1 兆美元，
其中 AI 伺服器產值將躍升至近 9,200 億美元 YoY +90%，占比進
一步提高至 81%左右。

2. 2026 年全球 AI 伺服器出貨量預計年增 31% 
根據 TrendForce 最新 AI server 產業研究，近期超大型 CSP、

採購意願明顯提高，且 Google、AWS 新一代 ASIC 平台於 2026
下半年陸續放量，加上中系業者擴大採用本地 AI 方案以滿足雲端
大型語言模型(LLM) AI 需求，促使 TrendForce 上調 2026 年 AI 
server 出貨年成長幅度，從原先的 28%更新為近 31%。北美五大

建 AI 資料中心、GPU 叢集、液冷散熱等新一代基礎設施，以應對
生成式 AI 及大型模型快速成長帶來的算力需求。其中，Google 
2026 至 2027 年都將側重自家 TPU 晶片發展，2027 年出貨量有
望繼續高速成長。AWS 2026 年以 NVIDIA GB300 作為 GPU AI 
server 主力，自研 ASIC 也穩定出貨，預期至 2027 年量能將再擴
大。Meta 2026 年主要採用 NVIDIA GB/VR、AMD Helios 整櫃式
方案，自研 ASIC 布局預計至 2027 年將明顯加速，為該公司長期
降低 AI 基礎設施成本、提升推論效率的重要策略。

於 1Q27 放量，ASP 提升約 50%至接近翻倍，AMD 專案亦將持續
出貨，再加上 ASIC 業務預計明年將有雙位數的成長，預估 2027
年 AI 伺服器營收將維持高雙位數年增；通用型伺服器 2027 年出
貨量與營收亦將維持雙位數增長。預估 2027 年伺服器佔總營收比
重將進一步攀升至 85%~90%，其中 AI 伺服器佔比將突破 80%。
而筆電則受記憶體價格居高不下影響，預估 2027 年出貨量將年減
高個位數至 10%，營收佔比將降至個位數百分比。資本支出方面，
今年前三季資本支出為 346 億元，公司先前宣布終止友達華亞廠
收購案，將轉向擴大海外佈局，預估 2027 年資本支出將略增至
400~450 億元，主要投入美國加州與泰國廠區，以支應未來 12~24
個月 CSP 客戶明確的產能預留需求。預估公司 2027 全年營收 5.85
兆，YoY +42% ，毛利率 4.37%，營利率 2.79%，稅後淨利 1278
億，全年稅後 EPS 31.86 元。

Update
Realtek Semiconductor  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Realtek remains relatively resilient despite sluggish PC demand, supported by its
diversified product portfolio and platform agnostic strategy. Near-term customer
inventory adjustments appear to reflect shipment timing rather than underlying
demand weakness. Meanwhile, elevated legacy DRAM prices continue to pressure
device BOM costs, potentially delaying broader adoption of edge Al and other

consumer applications
Looking ahead, edge server and edge Al represent structural growth opportunities,
supported by ongoing development in high-speed SerDes and integrated Al
solutions. Realtek also sees opportunities to strengthen its position in higher-end TV
SoCs amid competition. A normalization in memory prices could provide an
additional catalyst for downstream demand recovery.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Daniel Yen, CFA
Equity Analyst

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Realtek Semiconductor (2379.TW)
Base case, residual income model. We assume a cost of equity of 9.6%, an intermediate growth
rate of 7.3%, payout ratio of 79%, and terminal growth rate of 4.5%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger-than-expected Wi-Fi growth
n Faster-than-expected new product growth
n Faster-than-expected adoption for auto Ethernet
n Fading PC semi inventory corrections
Risks to Downside
n Weaker-than-expected Wi-Fi growth

n Slower-than-expected new product growth
n Slower-than-expected margin recovery given ongoing pricing competition
n Further TV SoC project losses in China
n Slower-than-expected adoption for auto Ethernet

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 3Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為五大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)34%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)37%、資訊產品(如筆電)7%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)7%、車載 15%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營業費用較高：矽力杰公布 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，
毛利率 47.58%，稅後獲利 9.09 億元(-42%QoQ，+34%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.31 元，
財報不如預期，主要是營業費用規模偏高。矽力杰 3Q26 財報細項指標如下：(1) 
營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，此係來自資料中心訂單加持、車載上
升(新款晶片推出與市占率提升)、消費電子季增 14%(即便市場需求疲弱，但因公
司新款晶片推出，故反而仍能向上)、資通訊產品下滑(因記憶體報價上漲，推高終
端售價而影響消費力道)。(2) 毛利率由 2Q26 的 47.03%微增至 47.42%，符合公
司原預期。(3) 營業費用 24.78 億元，季增達 19%，主要是銷售費用、資料中心應
用研發費用、3Q26 應屆畢業生招募人力增加。(4) 業外損失約 1100 萬元，相較
2Q26 業外收益 9.58 億元出現明顯差異，主要是前期有加特蘭微電子的再評價增
值利益產生。(5) 存貨 70.36 億元，季增 23%，存貨天數 150 天，達到歷史上緣位
置，公司表示係因封測供給吃緊，導致封測交期拉長(部分達到六個月)，因此 wafer bank
囤積在封測廠，導致庫存水位增加。

### Page 3

4Q26 營收持平到小增，略有不如預期：矽力杰預期(1) 4Q26 營收持平到小增(研
究部原預估 QoQ+5%~6%)，其中營運動能仍以資料中心為成長主軸，不過消費
電子市況相當疲弱(公司推測可能與客戶因應原物料與供應鏈不確定性，而提前出
貨，導致 4Q26 拉貨動能驟減)，包括手機與 TV 均顯弱勢。(2) 毛利率隨產品結構
變化、Gen4 製程平台滲透率提升至 20%，使得毛利率應能優於 3Q26 水準。
(3)4Q26~1H27 營業費用建議以 3Q26 的水準計算。研究部預估矽力杰 4Q26 營
收 74.29 億元(+2%QoQ，+38%YoY)，毛利率 47.84%，稅後獲利 10.5 億元
(+16%QoQ，+30%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.67 元。 
2027 年營收成長性落於 YoY+20%~+30%上緣，反映成長力道延續：矽力杰維持
預期資料中心、車載與為主要成長推手(兩者 2027 年營收總計 YoY+>50%)，並給
予更明確的營收比重目標值：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營
收比重 17%，2027 年底預計可達 25%。公司資料中心三大項目比重依序為光通
訊>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重
雖較低，不過主板業務(包括控制晶片與 SPS)的新款晶片目前客戶均已處驗證中，
預計 2027 年將放量(公司表是北美客戶的 SPS 需求快於中國)。公司表示儘管近期
資料中心有諸多因素干擾，不過客戶針對明年訂單需求仍相當穩固。(2) 車載營收
比重可望由現階段 15%，攀升至 2027 年的 20%(原預估 15%~20%)，主要動能
來自於 content value 提升(由 200~200USD 往 270~300USD 邁進，係來自 MCU
與高電壓/大電流晶片出貨)、中國電動車的海外銷售攀升。(3) 公司期待隨 Gen4
製程平台 2027 年滲透率達 50%、Gen5 製程平台量產與產品結構持續往資料中
心、車載布局，對 2027 年毛利率>50%有信心。不過研究部認為矽力杰過往毛利
率容易受到存貨損失調整(因庫齡政策)影響，又製造成本上漲壓力延續，故雖預估
2027 年毛利率趨勢向上，但上升的幅度以較保守的態度推估。

維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 255.51 億元(+36%YoY)，毛利率
47.6%，稅後獲利 42.98 億元(+72%YoY)，稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 338.3 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.98%，稅後獲利 60.32 億
元(+40%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營
運拓展，研究部維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

## Document 212: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

3Q26 Review: 4 분기 실적 반등 기대감 Up  
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 600,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 292,500 원(10/29)

 동사의 3분기 실적은 매출액 45.1조원(-3.4%yoy), 영업이익 2.68조원
(+5.5%yoy, OPM 5.9%), 지배이익 1.7조원(-24.6%yoy)을 기록하며 시장 
컨센서스를 상회. 도매 판매 감소, 원가 부담 증가 영향 있었으나 판매보증
충당금 축소와 원가 절감 활동을 통해 수익성 방어 
 실적 증감 요인: 영업이익 증감 요인은 물량 -1.11조원, 믹스/인센티브 -
0.57조원, 금융 -0.11조원, 환율 +1.28조원, 기타 +0.64조원 등. 원자재 
가격 상승으로 5천억원의 비용 증가 요인 있었으나 위기 대응 원가 절감 
활동으로 대부분 상쇄할 수 있었음. 전분기 대비 기말환율 급락함에 따라 
판매보증충당금 지난 해 1.26조원에서 340억원으로 약 1.23조원 감소. 판
보충 효과는 일회성에 그치겠지만 원가 절감 효과 컸던 점 긍정적  
 금융 부문: 금융부문 매출액은 전년비 2.8% 증가했지만 영업이익은 26% 
급감. 장기 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 증가와 기업금융 충당금 적립, 중고
차 관련 비용 확대가 수익성 악화의 주요 원인. 중고차 관련 비용은 EV 리
스 반납 차량의 매각 손실 확대에 기인한 것 
 4분기 전망: 동사는 국내 공장 정상화와 아반떼, 투싼, GV90, GV80 HEV 
등 주요 신차 효과를 통해 4분기 생산 확대와 주요 수출 시장 공급 회복이 
가능할 것으로 전망하였음. 원가 부담은 지속되나 물량 감소에 따른 영향에
서는 벗어날 수 있을 것. 원자재 가격 상승에 대응해 원가절감 프로그램 확
대와 신차 출시를 통한 인센티브 정상화를 통해 수익성 개선 지속할 계획 
 연간 가이던스 하향 조정: 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 기존 416만대에서 
400만대로 하향 조정. 매출 성장률은 +1~2%, OPM 가이던스는 기존 
6.3~7.3%에서 5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정. 자동차 부문 투자 계획은 17.8조
원에서 16.0조원으로 축소. 핵심 R&D 투자는 유지하되 Capex 집행 시기 
및 우선 순위 조정할 계획 
 4분기 실적 반등 전망: 4분기 주요 신차(투싼, 아반떼, GV80 HEV, GV90) 
출시 본격화로 3분기까지의 부진을 만회할 수 있을 것으로 기대. 원가 부담 
은 비용 절감 활동을 통해 충분히 만회가 가능한 것으로 3분기 실적을 통
해 확인됨. 본격적인 주가 반등을 위해서는 판매 회복 이상의 주가 트리거 
필요할 것으로 보이나, 4분기 반전의 실마리가 나타나고 있다는 점에 주목. 
동사에 대한 투자의견 매수와 목표주가 60만원을 유지함

시가총액(십억원) 59,514

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12 개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

## Document 225: [005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적현대차(005380) 저점을 받쳐줄 실적저점을 받쳐줄 실적 - 기업.pdf

목표주가 500,000

현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
292,500 
자동차업종

◼ 3Q26 비용절감/환율 효과로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현 
◼ 2027년 물량–환율 영향 상쇄되며 2H27 이익체력 개선 본격화 예상 
◼ 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등에 주목

투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 50만원 하향(-6%) 
현대차 TP 50만원(시총 102조원)은 12MF EPS 41,978원에 Target PER 12배
적용하여 산출. 제한적인 실적 기대 반영해 타겟 밸류 하향함에 따라 TP 조정  
우려 대비 양호한 3Q26 실적으로 주가 하방 부담 완화. 주가의 추세적 상승 
위해서는 1) Physical AI(로봇/SDV) 경쟁력, 2) 주요국 점유율 반등 확인돼야 할 
것. 기저효과에 따른 물량 개선이 주가 저점 받쳐줄 것이나, 현재 밸류(2027년 
PER 9.1배vs 글로벌 피어 6.1배)에서 추세 상승 이끌긴 어려울 것이기 때문. 
이에, 자동차 본업 외 1) 한/미국 Physical AI 투자 계획 및 2) BD 공급망 구축 
/양산 가시화에 따른 주가 반등 기대

3Q26 매출 45조(YoY -3%, 이하 YoY), OP 2.7조(+6%), OPM 5.9% 시현 
3Q26 OP 추정(2.5조)/컨센(2.4조) 상회. 파업에 따른 생산/판매 차질 영향으로 
기대 제한적이었으나, 비용 절감/환율 영향으로 우려 대비 양호한 실적 시현. 
[자동차] 매출 33조(-10%), OP 2.1조(+25%), OPM 6.5%. 도매 판매 92.1만대
(-11%). 판매 부진과 원자재 부담에도 불구, 환효과(+1.3조)/비용절감(+0.5조)
로 실적 방어. 이외 YoY 변수별 영향은 물량(-1.1조)/믹스(-0.6조)/원자재(-
0.5조). [금융] 매출 9.2조(+28%), OP 4,710억(-18%), OPM 5.1%, [기타] 매출 
2.9조(+2%), OP 712억(-72%), OPM 2.5% 기록

2027년 매출 191조(YoY +2%, 이하 YoY), OP 12.7조(+15%), OPM 6.6% 전망 
기저효과 바탕으로 연결 도매 물량 YoY +5% 증가 예상하나, 원달러 환율 -
4% 하락(가정 환율 1,380원)하며 1H27 → 2H27로가며 이익체력 개선 예상 
(단위: 십억원, %)

### Page 5

DAISHIN SECURITIES

5  
  현대차 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스콜 QnA 
Q. 금융부문 환율 변동에 따른 4Q26 매출 및 손익 영향 
- 금융부문 환율 영향은 현대차 실적에 미치는 환율 영향과 동일한 방향으로 발생

Q. 3Q26 금융부문 수익성 악화 요인 
- 장기금리 상승에 따른 조달 및 이자비용 증가가 주요인. 자산/부채 만기 매칭에 따른 
금리 상승 영향 반영 
- EV 중심 중고차 가격지수 소폭 하락 및 차량 반환에 따른 매각손실, 금리 상승에 따
른 연체율 및 신용비용 상승 
- 차량 판매가격 인하 영향은 없으며, 3Q26 도매 판매 감소에 따른 금융 영업수익 영향 
불가피했음

Q. 향후 주주환원 정책에서 배당 및 자사주 매입 우선순위 
- 기존 밸류업 정책: 최소 DPS 1만원/TPR 35% 제시. 연간 배당 약 2.6조원 → TPR 
35% 충족 위한 잔여분 자사주 매입/소각 활용 
- 경영환경 변화 고려하여 기존 밸류업 프로그램 전반의 주주가치 제고 방안 재검토중 
- 새로운 정책 확정 및 주주 소통 전까지 기존 약속인 최소 DPS 1만원 유지할 계획 
- 시장의 배당 및 자사주 매입 간 균형 요구를 인지하고 있으며, 주주 의견 수렴 후 주
주가치 제고 계획 공유 예정

Q. 3Q26 영업이익 증감 요인 중 기타 +6,400억 발생 원인 
- 월별 컨틴전시 활동을 통한 비용 최적화 및 재료비 절감 지속 추진 
- 4Q26 원가 절감 효과는 3Q26 대비 확대 예상

Q. 3Q26 영업외손익 감소 원인 
- 3Q26 환율 급락으로 외화 매출채권 등 외화자산 관련 외화환산손실 및 외환거래손실 
발생 
- 매출채권 인식 시점과 회수 시점 간 환율 차이에 따른 외환차손 발생 
- 지분법 대상 기업들의 3Q26 실적 부진에 따른 지분법손익 감소도 일부 영향. 대부분 
외환 관련 손실에서 발생

Q. 중국 OEM 국내 진출 확대에 따른 대응 및 신차/가격 전략 
- 중국 OEM 및 테슬라의 국내 M/S 확대 대응중. 다만, 잦은 가격 조정은 중고차 잔존
가치 및 고객가치 훼손할 수 있어 지양 
- 현대캐피탈의 캡티브 금융 경쟁력 활용. 잔존가치 유예할부 등을 통해 월 납입금을 낮
춰 중국 OEM 대비 경쟁력 확보 
- 서비스 네트워크 기반으로 경쟁사 대비 경쟁력 확보 노력 
- 국내 신차: 4Q26 아반떼 HEV, 투싼, GV90, G80 HEV, 싼타페 FL(2026.12월), G90 
FL 등 출시 예정 
- 북미: 4Q26 투싼, GV80 HEV 출시 및 27년 초 GV90 출시 예상 
- 유럽: 아이오닉3 판매 개시, 4Q26 투싼/베이온 등 주요 모델 출시 예정 
- 중국: 아이오닉 V / 인도: 베이온, 아이오닉 P 등 출시 예정 
- 신차 출시 계획 연도별로 다수 예정. 가격은 원가/경쟁사/소비자 가격 수용성 등을 고
려해 결정

Q. 3Q26 법인세비용 증가 원인 및 4Q26 전망

- 4Q26 법인세 수준은 QoQ 큰 변동 없을 것으로 전망

## Document 262: AI交易信仰动摇，纳指期货跌超3%，英伟达盘前跌超4%，金银大跌，避险资金涌入美债

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI交易信仰动摇，纳指期货跌超3%，英伟达盘前跌超4%，金银大跌，避险资金涌入美债.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美股股指期货跌幅扩⼤，纳指期货跌超 3% ，标普 500 指数期货跌超 1% 。科技股延续跌势，
英伟达跌超 4% ， Anthropic 下跌 3% ； AMD 跌近 5% ，闪迪跌超 5% 。现货⻩⾦跌破 3500 美
元 / 盎司关⼝，报 3495.49 美元 / 盎司，为去年 9 ⽉以来最低⽔平，较⾼点累计回撤将超过
37.5% 。基准 10 年期美债收益率下降 4 个基点⾄ 4.89% 。
摘要：
围绕⼈⼯智能热潮能否持续的疑虑正在重塑全球资产定价，投资者从科技股⼤举撤离，资⾦涌
向国债与美元避险。
11 ⽉ 25 ⽇，美股股指期货跌幅扩⼤，纳指期货跌超 3% ，标普 500 指数期货跌超 1% 。科技股延续
跌势，英伟达跌超 4% ， Anthropic 下跌 3% ； AMD 跌近 5% ，闪迪跌超 5% 。
避险需求推动美债上涨，基准 10 年期美债收益率下降 4 个基点⾄ 4.89% ，⽇本 10 年期国债收益率
同步下⾏ 7.5 个基点⾄ 2.645% 。现货⻩⾦跌破 3500 美元 / 盎司关⼝，报 3495.49 美元 / 盎司，为去

年 9 ⽉以来最低⽔平，较⾼点累计回撤将超过 37.5% 。现货⽩银跌破 40 美元 / 盎司关⼝，报 39.99
美元 / 盎司，创 2025 年 9 ⽉以来最低⽔平，较今年⾼点回落逾 67% 。
市场情绪在过去三个交易⽇内剧烈摇摆。上周五 AI 交易崩塌引发抛售，周⼀英伟达 CEO ⻩仁勋
回归掌舵带动股指反弹，但乐观情绪转瞬即逝，投资者次⽇便重启撤离。费城半导体指数周⼆
重挫 7.9% ，较 6 ⽉⾼点累计下跌约 40% 。市场的下⼀个焦点是周三公布的美国通胀数据，该数据
可能影响美联储的政策路径。
波动加剧，市场在寻找 AI 估值的锚
美股股指期货跌幅扩⼤，纳指期货跌超 2% ，标普 500 指数期货跌超 1% 。科技股延续跌势，英伟
达跌超 4% ， Anthropic 下跌 3% ； AMD 跌近 5% ，闪迪跌超 5% 。
美股股指期货跌幅扩⼤，纳指期货跌超 3% ，标普 500 指数期货跌超 1% 。科技股延续跌势，英
伟达跌超 4% ， Anthropic 下跌 3% ； AMD 跌近 5% ，闪迪跌超 5% 。

### Page 2

本轮调整的核⼼⽭盾在于，投资者开始质疑 AI 基础设施投资的可持续性。
“ 市场⼀直在围绕英伟达的股价波动，因为它显然是 AI 繁荣与萧条情景的关键， ”AT Global
Markets ⾸席市场分析师 Nick Twidale 表⽰， “ 我们仍处在⾼度波动的市场中，并且倾向于出现调
整，市场情绪明显每天都在摇摆。 ”
美债市场的⾛势印证了⻛险偏好的收缩。基准 10 年期美债收益率在纽约时段已经下⾏，亚洲时
段进⼀步扩⼤跌幅，显⽰资⾦正从股票流向固定收益资产。美元同步⾛强，成为本轮避险交易
的另⼀受益⽅。
据彭博报道， MSCI 全球指数下跌 0.4% ， 10 个交易⽇内第⼋次收跌。这⼀数据表明，调整并⾮局
限于 AI 相关板块，⽽是正在向更⼴泛的市场蔓延。
芯⽚股⾸当其冲，跌幅折射预期修正
半导体板块是这场抛售的中⼼。
SK 海⼒⼠ 11% 的单⽇跌幅和三星电⼦ 10% 的跌幅，在韩国市场形成了显著的指数拖累，也解释
了 KOSPI 指数 6.16% 的深度回调。中国台湾市场 4.8% 的跌幅同样指向芯⽚产业链的集中承压。

两地合计构成了 MSCI 亚太指数 2.1% 跌幅的主要来源。

### Page 3

费 城 半 导 体 指 数 较 6 ⽉ ⾼ 点 回 撤 约 40% ， 这 ⼀ 幅 度 在 技 术 上 接 近 熊 市 区 间 。 据 彭 博 报 道 ，
Anthropic PBC 的交易价格已跌破 IPO 发⾏价，进⼀步打击了市场对 AI 相关资产的信⼼。
“ 市场正在告诉我们，投资者不相信这样的投资规模能够按照此前指引推进，⾄少不是以⼀种可
持续的⽅式， ” 瑞讯银⾏亚洲⾸席投资官 Dong Chen 在彭博电视上表⽰。
戴尔科技和惠普在盘后交易中⼩幅上涨，此前两家公司发布了乐观的业绩指引。这显⽰出在整
体疲软的科技板块中，具备实际盈利⽀撑的硬件⼚商仍获得部分资⾦⻘睐。

⼤宗商品承压，油价⾦价同步⾛弱
避险情绪并未全⾯利好⼤宗商品，原油和⻩⾦双双⾛低。
布伦特原油下跌超过 1.5% ⾄每桶 90 美元，交易员在评估供应增加的迹象与霍尔⽊兹海峡周边船
只遇袭事件升级之间的影响。⻩⾦下跌 1.1% ⾄每盎司 3516 美元，⼋个交易⽇内第七次收跌，传
统避险资产的光环在本轮波动中明显减弱。
这⼀组合反映出市场的⽭盾⼼态：投资者既担忧 AI 叙事动摇带来的⻛险资产回撤，⼜未完全转向
传统的避险配置。资⾦流向更集中于美债和美元。
市场焦点仍在 AI 与美联储
Neumann 表⽰，年底前投资者的注意⼒仍然集中在 AI 和美联储⾝上。
据彭博策略师 Mark Cranfield 分析，对股票投资者⽽⾔，这看起来将是⼜⼀个紧张的交易时段，
市场夹在变得更具吸引⼒的估值与 AI 主题之间激烈的轮动之中。

对于投资者⽽⾔，接下来的关键变量有两个：周三的美国通胀数据将提供美联储政策⾛向的线
索，⽽ AI 板块能否⽌跌，取决于市场是否愿意重新相信此前的资本开⽀指引。在此之前，波动可
能仍将是市场的主旋律。
摩根⼤通美国⾼评级信贷策略主管 Nathaniel Rosenbaum 预计， AI 相关借贷将迎来⼜⼀个繁忙的
年份，但发⾏量的增⻓可能在 2027 年⻅顶。

## Document 265: AI制药终于开始省时间了

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI制药终于开始省时间了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

早期药物发现周期从 3 ⾄ 4 年缩短⾄ 2.5 年以内，临床研究报告的 90% 由 AI 起草，审阅时间减
少 25% 。这是⼤型药企⾸次以量化数据证明， AI 不再只是实验室⾥的概念，⽽是开始真实
地压缩研发时间与成本。摩根⼤通认为，在⼯具平⺠化的背景下，拥有数据资产与实验能
⼒的企业才具备可持续的竞争壁垒。
摘要：
据追⻛交易台，摩根⼤通在最新发布的研报中指出，诺华在 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇的投资者⽇上⾸次系统披
露了 AI 在药物研发中的实际成效：早期药物发现周期从 3 ⾄ 4 年缩短⾄ 2.5 年以内，临床研究报告
的 90% 由 AI 起草，审阅时间减少 25% 。这是⼤型药企⾸次以量化数据证明， AI 不再只是实验室
⾥的概念，⽽是开始真实地压缩研发时间与成本。摩根⼤通认为，这⼀信号对 AI 制药（ AIDD ）
投资主题构成建设性⽀撑。
诺华管理层同时给出了审慎的⼀⾯：许多 AI 设计的分⼦仍在基础细胞测试中失败， AI 尚未被证明
能提升⼈体临床试验的成功率。 11 ⽉ 23 ⽇，诺华 CEO Vas Narasimhan 在接受 CNBC 采访时表

⽰， AI 有望在未来 5 ⾄ 10 年内将药物发现与开发周期从 12 年压缩⾄ 8 年，并将⼈体试验成功率从
8% 提升⾄ 12% ，但他明确反对 “ ⼗年治愈癌症 ” 的乐观论调，认为复杂的⼈体⽣物学决定了这⼀
⽬标过于激进。
摩根⼤通的解读是，AIDD 的实际收益可能落在科技界的乐观预期与制药界的谨慎判断之间。研
报强调，算⼒与开源⼤模型正在让 AI 制药⼯具趋于同质化，真正的护城河是私有数据 —— 诺华
依托其 Data42 平台积累了超过 150PB 、历时⼗年构建的实验室数据。这⼀判断对中国 AIDD 标的
构成正⾯信号，摩根⼤通更⻘睐将 AI ⼯具与⼤规模湿实验室数据相结合的本⼟  AIDD 公司与 CXO
企业。
研发前端：发现周期压缩近四成
诺华披露的最直观数据来⾃药物发现环节。
管理层称，早期药物发现时间已从 3 ⾄ 4 年降⾄ 2.5 年以内，压缩幅度接近四成。这⼀环节历来是
制药⾏业最耗时、最依赖试错的部分，周期缩短意味着候选分⼦能够更早进⼊后续开发流程。
摩根⼤通在研报中写道，诺华管理层分享了明确迹象，显⽰ AI 已经在药物发现、临床试验和⼯

⼚运营三个环节削减成本、节省时间。该⾏认为， AI 正从早期的测试案例转向真实的研发应
⽤，为效率提升和管线加速创造了空间。
不过研报也如实记录了管理层的保留意⻅：许多由 AI 设计的分⼦依然⽆法通过基础细胞测试。这
意味着前端时间的节省尚未转化为分⼦质量的提升， AI 在发现环节的作⽤⽬前更多体现为筛选效
率，⽽⾮成功率。
临床与⽣产：报告起草与⼊组预测的量化改善
在临床试验环节，诺华给出的数据同样具体。 AI ⽬前承担了 90% 的临床研究报告起草⼯作，并将
报告审阅时间缩短 25% ；试验⼊组预测的准确率提升了 50% 。对于动辄涉及数千名患者、跨越
多个国家的⼤型临床试验⽽⾔，⼊组进度预测的准确性直接关系到试验周期与预算控制。
⽣产端的案例来⾃核药运输。在 Pluvicto 与 Lutathera 的配送中， AI ⽀持实现了接近 100% 的准时
交付。核药具有半衰期短、物流窗⼝窄的特性，准时率的⼩幅提升对商业化供应意义重⼤。管
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

理层还提到， AI 帮助削减了外部咨询费⽤，并降低了⼯⼚浪费。
这些环节的共同特征是流程标准化程度⾼、数据可得性强，因⽽成为 AI 最早落地的场景。摩根⼤
通认为，这类 “ 早期胜利 ” 表明 AI 正在获得真实的应⽤牵引⼒，但其价值集中在运营效率，⽽⾮科
学突破。
CEO 的平衡判断： 12 年变 8 年，成功率 8% 变 12%
诺华 CEO Vas Narasimhan 在 11 ⽉ 23 ⽇的 CNBC 采访中给出了更⻓期的时间表。他表⽰， AI 有望
在未来 5 ⾄ 10 年内将药物发现与开发的整体周期从 12 年缩短⾄ 8 年，并将⼈体临床试验的成功率
从 8% 提升⾄ 12% 。 Narasimhan 同时担任 Anthropic 董事会成员。
但他与部分科技界领袖的判断存在明显分歧。 Narasimhan 认为，受制于复杂的⼈体⽣物学， “ ⼗
年内治愈癌症 ” 的⽬标过于乐观。他提出， AIDD 的真实收益可能位于科技界的期望与制药界的谨
慎之间。
这⼀表态为市场提供了⼀个校准坐标： AI 对研发效率的改善已有数据⽀撑，但对疾病治愈率的跃

升仍需时间验证。对投资者⽽⾔，前者对应可⻅的成本节约与管线提速，后者则属于尚⽆法定
价的期权。
数据才是护城河，中国标的受益
摩根⼤通研报的核⼼判断是，诺华投资者⽇与此前的罗⽒制药⽇⼀样，指向对 AIDD 的建设性展
望，并更⻘睐拥有真实数据与湿实验室规模的企业。
管理层提到，算⼒与开源⼤语⾔模型可能正在使 AIDD ⼯具趋于均等化，真正的优势来⾃内部数
据。诺华依托 Data42 平台，积累了超过 150PB 、历时⼗年构建的私有实验室数据。摩根⼤通认
为，这对中国 AIDD 股票构成正⾯信号，该⾏看好将 AI ⼯具与⼤型实验室数据集相结合的本⼟
AIDD 公司与 CXO 企业。

### Page 3

摩根⼤通对 AIDD 的配置思路趋于精选：在⼯具平⺠化的背景下，拥有数据资产与实验能⼒的企
业才具备可持续的竞争壁垒。

## Document 331: First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail trade, September quarter 2026 - New
Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
09:19 November 23 2026 Share
The September quarter saw a larger than expected rise in spending, including firmness in
core categories.
September quarter retail sales
Retail sales (volume of goods sold): +2.2% (Prev: -0.4%)

### Page 2

Westpac f/c: 1.4%, Market: +0.5% 
Core retail sales (volume of goods sold): 1.4% (Prev: 0.8%)

Nominal retail sales: 2.4% (Prev: 1.0%)

Year to September
Volume of goods sold: 3.5%
Nominal sales: 7.0%

New Zealanders fill up the car and head back to the mall.
The September quarter retail sales report showed a sharp recovery in spending. Nominal spending
was up 2.4% over the quarter, and the amount of goods that New Zealanders are actually taking home
was up a solid 2.2%.
Today’s result was above our top of market forecast for a 1.4% quarterly rise, and well above the
median market forecast for +0.5%.
While the strong September quarter result is welcome news, we are taking it with a grain of salt. Much
of the rise in spending was related to fuel spending, which was boosted by the fall in fuel prices over
the September quarter, and fuel prices have step higher since then.
However, even excluding fuel, we have seen firmness in spending appetites. Core spending (excl. fuel
and motor vehicles) was up 1.4% over the September quarter and is 4.7% higher than this time last
year. 
Looking at the breakdown of spending, we did see a rise in grocery purchases. Meanwhile, spending in
discretionary categories was mixed – there has been lower spending on items like furnishings and
hardware, while spending on hospitality, electronics and recreational items is up. Overall, were left with
a picture of gradually improving spending appetites.

Outlook
Today’s report reinforces our expectations that the economy is moving into stronger phase. We expect
that spending levels will continue to firm through the holiday season and 2027. However, that recovery
is still likely to be gradual due to the combination of ongoing high inflation, a soft labour market and
rising interest rates. We also expect the recovery will be uneven, with those regions with strong links to
the agricultural and tourism sectors outperforming metro regions, like Auckland and Wellington.

Implications for GDP growth
We’re forecasting GDP growth of 0.6% in the September quarter. Today’s result was above
expectations. We’ll take a closer look at how our forecast for GDP growth is shaping up over the next
couple of weeks as additional data on September quarter activity is released.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2.2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%). 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect further 25bp increases at the three upcoming meetings (December 2026, January and 
March 2027), bringing the Fed Funds target range at 4.50% - 4.75%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand continues to widen. Retail sales barely increased in the first ten months of 2026, the 
crisis in the property sector continues and household confidence remains low. Yet new fiscal policy measures could help support investment 
and private consumption in the very short term. Deflationary pressures are persisting, given weakness in domestic demand. Energy prices and 
electronic goods prices are increasing, producer price inflation is accelerating (5.7% y/y in October), but consumer price inflation remains low 
(0.7% y/y).

EUROZONE 
The economy has shown resilience in the face of the energy shock: GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3, and the 1.0% growth carry-over for 2026 
supports our forecast of 1.1% for the year as a whole (compared to 1.3% in 2025) with the risk tilted to the upside. Growth is expected to 
strengthen to 1.6% in 2027, bolstered by increased spending on defence and infrastructure, as well as the global investment cycle in AI. Annual 
average inflation is thus expected to reach 3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), and 3.2% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, 
we anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause. That 
said, stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 2026 (following stagnation in Q2), weighed down by a decline in aeronautics production (delay in the 
delivery of inputs) but supported by service sector output and purchases of electric vehicles. Overall, growth is expected to slow to 0.3% in 
2026 (after 0.9% in 2025) before rebounding to 0.8% in 2027. In parallel, inflation should reach 2.5% in 2026 (compared with 0.9% in 2025), 
before edging down slightly in 2027 (2.4%).

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would accelerate to 1.6% in 2026 (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered both the energy price shock and domestic 
political volatility, boosted by domestic demand, notably investment. After a series of strong prints from Q1 to Q3, real GDP growth is expected 
to decelerate in the last quarter of the year, as higher energy prices should feed inflation. It should then slow to 1.3% in 2027 on the back of 
higher inflation and more restrictive monetary conditions. Inflation should climb to 3.8% in Q4 2026, peaking in Q1 2027 (at 4.4%) before 
gradually slowing. Still, it is set to remain stubbornly above target, leading to mounting second-round effects risks. In response, the BoE has 
finally raised its Bank Rate to 4.00% early November. If a second-rate hike looks likely in February, this is not, for now, our call and our baseline 
scenario projects that diminishing inflation will allow monetary easing to resume in the second half of 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank 
Rate down to 3.50% by year-end.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 0.9% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.0% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of 2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the US dollar. The dollar's structural downtrend is unlikely to 
resume until 2027, potentially reaching 1.20 by year-end 2027, although political and fiscal risks could weigh on the Euro next year. The 
USD/JPY appreciation is expected to continue, albeit at a more moderate pace, as the BoJ remains behind the curve despite its rate hikes. 
Conversely, GBP/USD would benefit from relative support, bolstered by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government and 
resilient growth.

Recent AI-related market stresses and new regulatory uncertainty are
raising fresh questions about the durability of the AI investment cycle. For
answers, we turn to Social Capital’s Chamath Palihapitiya and GS’ Eric
Sheridan and Miriam Wheeler as well as other GS credit and equity
research analysts to discuss the source of the current stresses, who holds
the risk in the AI ecosystem, where the outlook for ROI central to the AI

theme now stands, and how investors can best navigate the current
moment.
Chamath Palihapitiya, Founder and CEO, Social Capital
“[I see] a mismatch between promised data center capacity, actual capacity, and returns. That matters particularly for
neoclouds, which are making enormous commitments for GPUs, power, and data center capacity, while the demand needed to
support those commitments ultimately depends on end-customers earning a meaningful return on their AI spending.”

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 153

The AI investment cycle shows little sign of slowing as tech 
companies commit ever-larger sums to the AI buildout. But 
recent AI-related stresses in equity and credit markets, 
increasingly circular financing arrangements, and new 
regulatory uncertainty following the “blue tsunami” in the US 
midterm elections are raising fresh doubts about whether the 
current pace of spending can continue and the durability of 
the AI theme itself. So, how durable is the AI investment 
cycle, what could cause it to inflect, and what would a 
slowdown mean for companies and markets that have been 
so dominated by the theme? 
We first speak with Chamath Palihapitiya, Founder and CEO 
of Social Capital, to discuss the area of the AI ecosystem 
most under pressure: neoclouds. Their fundamental 
vulnerability, he explains, is a business model that uses 
speculative short-term revenue to support fixed, long-dated 
obligations. This, he says, leaves neoclouds dependent on 
the utilization rates of the data center capacity they’ve 
committed to deliver, and delivering that capacity in itself is 
mind-numbingly complex. But he stresses that the utilization 
hurdle is also daunting, as demand for compute must grow 
fast enough to outpace ongoing chip and model efficiency 
gains and support the capacity neoclouds have committed to 
build. He argues that achieving that demand growth will 
ultimately come down to large companies realizing 
meaningful ROI from their AI spending—which he thinks will 
ultimately happen but questions if it will do so quickly 
enough for investors to stay the course. All that said, he 
notes that all neoclouds are not created equal, with those 
who own the binding constraints of land, power, and shell 
better-positioned than those that lease them.      
GS lead Software analyst Gabriela Borges then digs deeper 
into the economics of neoclouds and of CoreWeave in 
particular, which has been at the epicenter of the recent 
volatility. She argues that CoreWeave’s true cost of debt is 
lower than what many investors assume and consistent with 
a still attractive excess return. And she believes that the rise 
in the price of CoreWeave’s credit default swaps (CDS)—one 
of the few liquid CDS data center instruments in the market 
today—is partly a reflection of investors hedging their 
infrastructure investments and sees a very low probability of 
further pressure on CoreWeave’s debt service obligations. 
She also notes that financing needs for non-IG issuers more 
broadly may be less than what investors expect. 
We then turn to Miriam Wheeler, GS Global Head of 
Leveraged Finance within Credit & Asset Finance in the 
Capital Solutions Group, for a first-hand perspective on what 
capital markets are telling us about investor appetite for such 
non-IG issuance. She points out that the pace of AI deals in 
the high yield market has slowed considerably amid investor 
fears about deal supply and the changing political backdrop. 
While she argues that data center deals can still get done in 
the high yield market, she expects the market to become 
increasingly bifurcated, with top-tier developers and projects 
likely continuing to have easier access to capital than others.  
GS Chief Credit Strategist Amanda Lynam then addresses 
broader credit market challenges to continuing to fund the AI 
buildout. She argues that while a higher cost of capital could 
be a gating factor for further capex, especially for the non-IG 
universe, the bigger issue is market saturation given the 
scale of IG debt issuance and rising off-balance sheet 
obligations that are increasingly giving rating agencies and 
credit investors pause. Asset allocators, she says, are 
increasingly balancing their overall AI exposure across a 
range of asset classes—including equities, syndicated credit, 
and private markets. As a result, she expects investors to be 
increasingly selective about where they take AI exposure, 
and at what price. And Lynam, like Palihapitiya, thinks that 
evidence of monetization will be key to mitigating downward 
pressure on ratings as well as sustaining investors’ 
willingness to continue to fund the AI buildout. 
So, the crucial question remains: will the substantial amount 
of AI capex generate sufficient returns and, if so, will these 
returns become visible before investors capitulate on the 
theme? We speak again with Eric Sheridan, GS lead US 
Internet and Interactive Entertainment analyst. He argues 
that troughs of disillusionment around return potential are a 
common feature of compute cycles and that the current 
episode is no different than other troughs that have occurred 
even in this cycle, though the stakes are undoubtedly higher 
as the dollars being spent have grown considerably. And he 
says that while ROI visibility never happens as fast as 
investors would like, hyperscalers have much higher revenue 
backlogs today relative to a few years ago and can “easily 
achieve a 15%+ return on invested capital”, in his view (GS 
US senior portfolio strategist Ryan Hammond sees an even 
higher revenue wall for the application layer to climb than the 
hyperscalers, but argues it is also potentially achievable).  
That said, Sheridan echoes Palihapitiya’s general view that if 
the demand for compute disappoints, access to capital 
becomes widely disrupted, or the AI ecosystem finds itself 
short of the power required for data center needs, “the 
current trough of disillusionment could become a more 
permanent state of contraction.” 
So, how should investors navigate the current trough? Our 
interviewees agree that investors should position for safety, 
with Wheeler seeing a continued focus among investors on 
going “up in credit quality.” And Palihapitiya and Sheridan 
explain that hyperscalers remain relatively well positioned to 
weather a potential slowdown in the AI investment cycle 
thanks to their strong balance sheets, diversified business 
models, and existing data center capacity that could become 
even more valuable if regulation ultimately constrains the AI 
buildout.    
Allison Nathan, Editor 
Email: allison.nathan@gs.com   
Tel: 212-357-7504   
Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC    
Is the AI investment cycle durable?

### Page 4

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 4 
Top of Mind Issue 153

Chamath Palihapitiya is Founder and CEO of Social Capital, a venture capital firm that invests 
in transformational technologies. He served as an early member of the senior executive team 
at Facebook and held leading roles at The Mayfield Fund, AOL, and Winamp. Below, he 
argues that the sustainability of the AI investment cycle ultimately hinges on end-customers 
seeing meaningful returns on their AI spend, which has yet to happen.  
The views stated herein are those of the interviewee and do not necessarily reflect those of Goldman Sachs.
Allison Nathan: Your recent worries 
about CoreWeave and neoclouds 
more broadly garnered considerable 
attention. What were you seeing 
that others were missing? 
Chamath Palihapitiya: Let me start 
by saying that I do not have a particular 
issue with CoreWeave or a position 
one way or the other. My concerns 
stem from my experience moving up and down the AI stack. 
Down the stack, I have spent significant time investing in land, 
power, and shell—the physical data center infrastructure—
because it was clear that power was becoming the binding 
constraint in the AI buildout. Up the stack, I started 8090—an 
AI-native software development platform—to understand how 
AI was translating into actual ROI because I was not convinced 
it was. Those experiences revealed a mismatch between 
promised data center capacity, actual capacity, and returns. 
That matters particularly for neoclouds, which are making 
enormous commitments for GPUs, power, and data center 
capacity, while the demand needed to support those 
commitments ultimately depends on end-customers earning a 
meaningful return on their AI spending.  
On the demand side, the hardest problem is change 
management. Companies start with sweeping ideas about AI-
related boosts to productivity and profitability, but the lived 
reality is that AI projects are difficult to implement. People in 
the middle ranks of firms are often skeptical or afraid of the 
technology, so they push back on implementation, significantly 
slowing diffusion. And even when AI generates productivity 
gains, they are often lost elsewhere in the value chain; AI may 
save an employee two hours a day, but that does not 
necessarily mean the employer gains two hours of productive 
output. So, the number of companies that can attribute 
earnings or profitability gains to AI is essentially zero today.  
On the supply side, my co-founder and I have now accumulated 
six gigawatts of powered assets in the US, so I see first-hand 
the mind-numbing complexity of operationalizing assets, 
involving everything from acquiring mineral rights and 
conducting environmental studies, to discovering that the 
concrete pads do not fit or the turbine company is backlogged, 
and the list goes on.   
So, companies are promising significant data center capacity, 
but the actual delivery volume is low, and the surface area for 
ROI is lower still. That is what concerns me, and the market has 
now also begun to sniff this out judging by the recent blowout 
in credit default swap (CDS) spreads and increased volatility 
more broadly for several companies involved in the AI buildout.      
Allison Nathan: What about neoclouds’ business model 
makes end-customer ROI so important? 
Chamath Palihapitiya: Neoclouds have the opposite funding 
model of a bank. Banks borrow short and lend long; neoclouds 
use speculative short-term revenue to support long-dated, fixed 
obligations. The best analogy for how the neocloud business 
works—made by both Ken Griffin and Sridhar Ramaswamy—is 
the airline industry. GPUs are the engine. The data center is the 
airplane shell. Powered land is the airport. Compute is the seat, 
sold to companies by the hour. And like an empty airline seat, 
an unsold hour of compute is perishable. So, the business 
fundamentally depends on utilization. An airline generally needs 
to operate at 80-85% utilization to break even, with margins and 
earnings accruing above that level. For neoclouds, the 
comparable figure varies by operator and GPU class but is 
roughly 70-75%. So, the upper portion of utilization is where 
neoclouds actually make money.  
This is where end-customer ROI becomes so important. Silicon 
and model efficiency may improve the economics of AI for 
customers, but not necessarily for neoclouds. An operator that 
has made long-dated commitments to a particular GPU class 
cannot simply upgrade its hardware every year when Nvidia 
releases a newer version because it must amortize what it 
bought. And if smaller models can perform the same work as 
bigger models, customers will need fewer tokens and less 
compute to achieve the same result. So, demand has to grow 
enough to offset these efficiency gains and support the 
capacity neoclouds have committed to building.   
Crucially, that demand must be ROI-linked. If meaningful ROI 
starts to emerge for end-customers, particularly large 
enterprises, AI spending will increase materially. So, token 
consumption and utilization will spike off the charts. In that 
scenario, everybody prints—the neoclouds, hyperscalers, 
chipmakers, and model companies. That is the bull case for the 
neoclouds. The bear case is largely the status quo: companies 
continue spending on AI, but most of that spending remains in 
the experimentation bucket rather than becoming large, 
recurring enterprise commitments. In that scenario, token 
consumption never explodes, leaving neoclouds in the 75-85% 
utilization gray zone in which they are making money, but not 
enough to pay off their debts before their hardware becomes 
obsolete. Nothing needs to get much worse for that outcome 
to materialize. 
Allison Nathan: Why has meaningful ROI yet to emerge 
even at organizations that are using AI at scale? 
Chamath Palihapitiya: Increased token consumption and the 
rising use of AI agents do not necessarily make a firm more 
profitable. In fact, it may decrease profits because more agents 
Interview with Chamath Palihapitiya

### Page 5

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 5 
Top of Mind Issue 153

running more work may increase aggregate costs even as the 
cost per token declines. This speaks to the other big problem at 
the end-customer level: the main purveyors of AI sell it as 
inventory rather than as an outcome, because their business 
model is selling cost per million tokens. In that sense, AI 
resembles the internet in 1999, when large companies sold 
advertising inventory on a cost-per-thousand basis before 
Google came along and shifted the business model toward cost 
per click. The AI industry will likely undergo a similar transition. 
Eventually, a company will sell the technology on a cost-per-
outcome basis, allowing enterprises to directly assess how 
much value their spending generated. But that business model 
does not exist yet.   
Allison Nathan: If the ROI never materializes, who will bear 
the losses? 
Chamath Palihapitiya: Neoclouds have been at the center of 
concerns, but the special purpose vehicles (SPVs) used to 
finance much of the data center buildout are well isolated and 
structured to minimize risk. That said, some neoclouds are 
more at risk. One simple and insightful way to assess the 
relative risk is to ask an AI model to review all of the public 
disclosures each neocloud has made about buildout capacity 
and compare that with its obligations and take-or-pay customer 
commitments to determine whether it can generate enough 
revenue to service those obligations, and then rank the 
neoclouds from most to least at risk. Another way to assess 
risk is to examine what each neocloud actually owns. Operators 
that own the binding constraints of land, power, and shell have 
real leverage and can generate value for equity holders even if 
they run into trouble. But if all they have are leases, they are 
essentially middlemen. That is a highly problematic business 
model because it requires operational perfection.   
Ultimately, though, all roads lead back to the investment grade 
(IG) issuers funding the capex buildout: the hyperscalers. The 
SPVs may isolate risk effectively, but most are underpinned by 
an IG wrapper somewhere in the system. It is striking that 
many hyperscalers have shifted from being massively cash flow 
positive, buying back stock, and largely avoiding debt issuance 
to issuing debt and equity to finance the buildout. So, they have 
made an all-in bet that they can backstop the buildout because 
they believe the AI bull case will ultimately prove right. They will 
probably be right. The question is when.  
All the prognostications about the internet were right, but the 
timing was wrong, and the industry went through a years-long 
clearing process before finally finding its footing in 2010. But 
the hyperscalers can withstand a bump in the road even if 
some neoclouds cannot because they have incredible 
underlying businesses and pristine balance sheets. They are 
also protected because they will not keep providing an IG wrap 
to neoclouds that fail to execute, which can happen in many 
ways: poorly executed technology, missed utilization targets, or 
failure to deliver or cool a data center properly. Microsoft 
recently withdrew from a planned data center project in Europe 
owing to execution issues, and some US operators have 
suffered the same fate. And even if some neoclouds fail, 
hyperscalers can just absorb the critical assets, providing 
another form of protection. I suspect that in five years, the 
hyperscalers will end up being massive direct owners of land, 
power, and shell for this reason. So, the hyperscalers will be 
fine in the end. 
Allison Nathan: With the AI trade already under pressure, 
how much time does the industry have to show that end-
customer ROI can justify the capex? 
Chamath Palihapitiya: The next 18 months are critical. We will 
not have the final answer by then, but we will have set the 
table for a clearing process that distinguishes sustainable AI 
business models and infrastructure commitments from 
unsustainable ones. The reason is that a larger share of the 
major businesses across the AI stack will come under public 
market scrutiny, giving investors a clearer view of how these 
companies generate revenue and whether their business 
models can scale durably.  
The key question is whether the major model providers will 
remain primarily consumption-based businesses or shift toward 
more outcome-based business models. One useful indicator to 
watch will be the revenue mix between à la carte token 
consumption and subscriptions. A subscription is not the same 
as outcome-based pricing, but it is directionally closer. The next 
18 months should also provide greater clarity on whether open-
source models will cannibalize token consumption by delivering 
the same unit of work at a structurally lower cost, and whether 
model providers move up the stack, start competing with their 
customers, and capture application-layer revenue. As those 
answers emerge, public market investors across the ecosystem 
will be able to make more definitive judgments about the 
underlying business models, creating the conditions for a 
clearing process. 
Allison Nathan: Ultimately, if end-customer ROI does not 
emerge, could we see a 1999-style reckoning?   
Chamath Palihapitiya: Probably not. The industry will more 
likely experience a period of rationalization, which could occur in 
a few different places. One is in the end-markets. Customers 
could refuse to increase spending unless and until they see 
clear returns. Another is in the debt markets. If lenders remain 
unconvinced by neoclouds’ utilization assumptions, or they fail 
to support those assumptions with specific evidence, the cost 
of capital will rise. The weakest credits could face borrowing 
costs approaching 20%, CDS spreads could approach 1000bp, 
and even IG borrowers could face near-double-digit funding 
costs. The cost of capital is the single most powerful forcing 
mechanism: if the AI ecosystem must finance itself at rates of 
12-20%, the market would be effectively telling the participants 
to stop fooling around and make the ROI case clear.  
None of that requires a cataclysmic outcome. A handful of 
neoclouds may just conclude that their business models are not 
viable and choose to sell assets or transfer leases. Significant 
demand for capacity exists, including from the hyperscalers, 
which remain extremely supply-constrained. So, if the financing 
markets wobble, those companies could become natural asset 
buyers, as we discussed. So, the more likely outcome is a 
correction and a redistribution of assets rather than a crash. But 
the underlying issue remains the same: someone still has to 
provide canonical, falsifiable proof of a direct link between AI 
spending and ROI. And nobody has done that yet.

### Page 6

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 6 
Top of Mind Issue 153

Q&A on recent stresses in neoclouds 
Q: What are neoclouds, and why has CoreWeave been at the center of volatility in these stocks? 
A: There are over 300 neoclouds, which are specialized providers of GPU compute. Most are relatively new to the market, and 
some, like CoreWeave, pivoted from using GPUs for bitcoin mining to using them for compute services. CoreWeave has attracted 
outsized attention because its business model sits at the center of investor concerns about the AI data center world and it operates 
this model at greater scale and with a more leveraged capital structure than most peers. Indeed, the company is scaling very 
quickly, with revenue on track to grow 150%+ in 2026, but in order to grow, it must first spend a very large amount of capex. In 
other words, the steeper CoreWeave’s future demand growth curve gets, the more capex the company has to put in the ground 
upfront to support it. And because the company is still scaling, its current fleet of GPUs does not generate enough cash to self-fund 
the next phase of growth, so it must raise debt.  
Q: What do CoreWeave’s financing needs look like, and what funding options are available to meet them? 
A: CoreWeave funds its projects through delayed draw term loans (DDTLs). CoreWeave will sign a specific customer contract, for 
example, to provide $11bn per year of compute services over four years (a $44bn total contract). Delivering on this would require 
around one gigawatt (GW) of new capacity to be brought online, which would carry a capex cost of $37.5bn today. CoreWeave can 
finance the majority of that through DDTLs (typically ~80%), with the balance covered by a combination of its own cash or customer 
prepayments. The DDTL goes through a rigorous credit assessment, allowing it to be rated in line with the credit rating of the end-
customer, and the debt is fully repaid within the term of the customer contract (in this case, four years). As CoreWeave scales, its 
installed base can fund new supply.  
How do CoreWeave’s DDTLs work?

Source: Goldman Sachs GIR.

Q: Are the concerns about CoreWeave’s financing needs warranted, or overblown?  
A: Two aspects of CoreWeave’s debt are often misinterpreted. The first is that CoreWeave’s credit default swap (CDS) is volatile. A 
higher CDS price typically signals a greater risk of default. However, we think CoreWeave’s CDS price has risen partly because 
investors are buying it as a hedge against existing infrastructure investments since its CDS is one of the few liquid data center CDS 
instruments in the market today. We see a very low probability of further pressure on CoreWeave’s debt service obligations, 
because its contracts are take-or-pay, the debt is only drawn when the data centers are ready to go live, and the debt is generally 
self-amortized within the life of the customer contract.  
The second aspect is that CoreWeave has $10bn in unsecured debt that it raised to support its broader corporate needs, which now 
trades at a 16% yield-to-worst. Investors may see this and conclude that the cost of all debt for CoreWeave is now 16%. However, 
our industry conversations suggest that CoreWeave’s DDTLs (which are secured)—its primary source of financing—price much 
lower. Even with this higher cost of debt, the contract economics still produce a relatively attractive IRR to WACC spread. And, 
importantly, we believe the probability that CoreWeave needs to raise more unsecured debt in the next five years is very low. 
Q: CoreWeave’s stresses have raised concerns about the health of the AI investment cycle. What do CoreWeave’s financing 
pressures signal about broader AI financing/the AI capex cycle?  
A: The cost of financing will be higher today than it was a few months ago. However, we think the market remains deep enough to 
support new issuance from IG issuers. Our credit strategists benchmark additional potential hyperscaler issuance against the largest 
issuers in the USD IG bond market today (i.e., US banks), and estimate that another $500bn of IG issuance could be supported. That 
said, they note that the scale and scope of AI-related gross debt extends well beyond the hyperscaler universe, underscoring the 
importance of managing exposure concentration as the multi-year issuance cycle unfolds.  
For non-IG issuers, the financing required may be smaller than investors expect. Levers to financing such as BYO-hardware or 
vendor prepayments reduce the need for external capital, and the installed base will generate cash as it matures. Non-traditional

sources also add capacity, including the $19tn private markets universe (which includes $4.5tn of capacity to be put to work, per 
Preqin) and Nvidia’s $500bn financing pool. 
Q: How much risk does a potentially stricter regulatory environment pose to the neoclouds? 
A: New challenges, such as local politics, are making it harder to scale new supply. And any delays would put some neoclouds at 
risk of failing to meet their customer commitments. The key factor to watch is how much of each neocloud’s planned capacity ramp 
is already contracted vs. still a work in progress, and how much operational flexibility they have to shift to new locations. 
Encouragingly, we find that data center operators have been planning for a shift in the regulatory environment for some time—the 
only people surprised by potential new regulatory hurdles are people not in the business of building data centers. But if building data 
centers does become harder, neoclouds with existing supply commitments or work-in-progress construction would likely become 
more valuable, not less.  
Q: What would have to happen for you to become more concerned about CoreWeave/the broader AI investment cycle? 
A: Two industry dynamics bear watching. The first is demand. For neoclouds or hyperscalers to earn a return, they must generate 
sufficient revenue. Our US internet analysts find that in order for hyperscalers to earn a 15% ROIC on their 2026-2027E capex 
investments, they need to generate $1.42tn in revenue cumulatively over 2028-2030, or roughly $500bn per year (see pgs. 12-13). 
This $500bn represents the training compute and the inference cost of goods sold (COGS) (i.e., the expense) for their AI customers, 
who in turn need to generate enough revenue from differentiated AI services to build sustainable businesses. So, there must be 
demand for those AI services. We think this demand will materialize as enterprises roll out AI more broadly and adopt more 
sophisticated agentic processes. However, if demand slows, we would be more concerned about the economics of the entire 
infrastructure software supply chain. 
The second is supply. Most industry participants tell us the compute environment is very supply constrained today. However, a 
large amount of capacity is set to come online in the next two years. So, the key will be whether demand remains strong enough 
for neoclouds to earn attractive returns on this new supply. If too much supply comes online, compute prices could fall. This is 
especially risky for neoclouds that sell on the spot market; as a point of comparison, we estimate that 95%+ of CoreWeave’s 
business is 4-5 year take-or-pay contracts. 
Gabriela Borges, Lead US Software Analyst

Miriam Wheeler is Global Head of Leveraged Finance within Credit & Asset Finance in the 
Capital Solutions Group at Goldman Sachs. Below, she argues that the high yield market for 
AI-related financing will likely become increasingly bifurcated, with up in quality a key theme. 
The interviewee is an employee of Goldman Sachs Global Banking & Markets and the views stated herein reflect those of the 
interviewee, not Goldman Sachs Research.
Ashley Rhodes: How have 
conditions in the AI financing space 
evolved? 
Miriam Wheeler: The year began with 
high yield investors underexposed to 
AI and navigating headwinds in the 
software sector, which provided a 
constructive, risk on backdrop for AI in 
the high yield market. With credit 
spreads broadly approaching all time tights, we saw heavy 
demand and most deals priced in the 6% to 7% all-in yield 
context without significant differentiation. Over the summer, 
that optimism gave way to increasing concern that the market 
would be flooded with supply. At one point the market was 
expecting $60bn of issuance this fall, which significantly 
pressured spreads. In addition, with the political backdrop 
changing, investors are also more focused on project level risks 
and more selective in their underwriting. Since the end of 
September we have seen supply contract dramatically in the 
non-IG market with no new deals coming over the last month.  
To put that in perspective, around 30 AI deals came to market 
over the past 12 months, with a new deal happening every 
other week—sometimes every week—during the summer. So, 
market sentiment has shifted substantially.      
Ashley Rhodes: Can a data center deal still get done? 
Miriam Wheeler: Yes. A deal with a strong and experienced 
developer, which has all of the necessary permits, and is 
appropriately structured with lease economics for the current 
market, could get done today. We think the market will favor 
transactions that are more manageable from a size perspective 
and that have IG wrappers. Investors will be particularly focused 
on making sure permits and local approvals are fully buttoned 
up.  
Ashley Rhodes: What risks do high yield investors face in 
these structures? 
Miriam Wheeler: The high yield market generally is financing 
two types of data centers. The first is data center deals that 
have non-investment grade tenants (for example CoreWeave or 
Jane Street). The second type is data centers with leases that 
have non-IG tenants with an IG tenant wrap or residual value 
guarantee, but where the lease has a termination option for late 
delivery. That wrapper effectively transforms non-IG tenant 
exposure into IG-like credit risk, provided the developer builds 
and delivers the facility on time, because these wrappers only 
become effective upon completion. Developers typically have a 
180-day grace period for late delivery, but if a project misses 
that outside date, the tenant has a termination option that 
allows them to walk away. 
Generally speaking, the deals in the IG market have been IG 
tenants and date certain leases whereas the deals in the high 
yield market have more project risk or higher beta tenants. The 
tail risk investors worry about is an extended, multi-year delay 
on a project in a pioneering market where the re-tenanting 
analysis is more complicated.  
That said, many existing data center projects that have been 
financed in the high yield market are now advanced in 
construction and nearing delivery. More broadly, hyperscalers 
remain extremely short on compute. So, if a project is running 
only slightly behind schedule, these tenants need the capacity 
so much that they are far more likely to work with the 
developer than pull the plug—especially now that capacity could 
become harder to build. From a macro perspective, the ability to 
finance long-term infrastructure backed by strong contracts 
amid an enduring supply-demand imbalance makes this a 
fundamentally compelling investment opportunity.    
 From a macro perspective, the ability to 
finance long-term infrastructure backed by 
strong contracts amid an enduring supply-
demand imbalance makes this a 
fundamentally compelling investment 
opportunity.”   
Ashley Rhodes: Is chip financing also inherently risky for 
credit investors given how rapidly the underlying hardware 
becomes obsolete? 
Miriam Wheeler: The chip space is very attractive for investors 
because of its short duration. GPU-backed facilities typically 
have a weighted average life of 3-5 years and fully amortize 
over that period, and the payments are supported by contracted 
cash flows that are often from an IG tenant. Those deals also 
often feature strong structural protections, with funds held in 
escrow until all delivery conditions are met and the commercial 
contract becomes effective. In that sense, investors are 
essentially underwriting derivative hyperscaler risk. The real 
challenge for that market is the sheer volume of supply. In a 
typical data center facility, the physical shell and power 
infrastructure account for only around 25% of the total cost, 
while the chips account for roughly 75%. That creates an 
enormous volume of hardware that requires financing, which 
ultimately could pressure spreads.  
Ashley Rhodes: Given the interconnectivity of chipmakers, 
data center developers, and tech companies in the 
financing ecosystem, is contagion a risk if one or a handful 
of companies come under pressure?  
Interview with Miriam Wheeler

### Page 9

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 9 
Top of Mind Issue 153

Miriam Wheeler: We are still in a market that is short supply. 
While there will be winners and losers, I am not particularly 
concerned about systemic contagion risk. The market will favor 
investors who can do the deep project level analysis and back 
the best projects. 
 I am not particularly concerned about 
systemic contagion risk.”   
Ashley Rhodes: Is the high asset-level leverage in many 
high yield financing structures a concern?  
Miriam Wheeler: Leverage is an important consideration when 
looking at refinancing risk in high yield data center financings. 
While these facilities are typically underpinned by 15- to 20-year 
leases, the bonds used to finance them often mature in five 
years, creating refinancing risk at the end of that period. If rates 
have risen or credit spreads have widened when that debt 
comes due, leverage would amplify the issue. The more debt 
an asset carries, the tighter its debt service coverage ratio 
(DSCR) becomes—meaning there is less cash flow cushion to 
service debt payments—which could limit the amount of new 
debt lenders are willing to provide.  
Ashley Rhodes: How do you expect high yield deal 
structures and credit quality to evolve from here? 
Miriam Wheeler: With the current volatile backdrop, we expect 
investors to be highly selective. The market will favor well 
capitalized developers, strong leases with high-quality tenants, 
and projects that benefit from having all of their required 
permits and approvals. With wider yields available in both the IG 
and non-IG markets, investors can hit their return hurdles while 
taking less risk. Structural complexity will likely come out of the 
market as investors move up in credit quality without needing 
to stretch. In the short-term, leases with non-IG tenants may 
remain more challenging. Developers with an unproven track 
record, limited equity, or inferior leases will likely struggle to 
access capital.  
Dispersion is a dominant theme in the credit markets generally 
and applies to the AI space as well. Projects that are 
substantially advanced and wrapped by IG tenants have seen 
far less spread volatility than deals with non-IG tenants or noise 
around their delivery schedule, which have much higher betas.  
We expect the high yield market to remain open. The secular 
demand for compute is simply too large, and the supply still 
hasn’t caught up with demand. But bifurcation will likely remain 
the defining theme in the high yield market, mirroring a dynamic 
that is playing out across the broader market landscape.    
 Bifurcation will likely remain the defining 
theme in the high yield market, mirroring a 
dynamic that is playing out across the broader 
market landscape.”   
Ashley Rhodes: What are you watching most closely to 
gauge how all of this will play out? 
Miriam Wheeler: We are watching three key dynamics. First, 
we are keeping a close eye on developments at the local policy 
level, particularly in Texas, where the Governor-Elect has 
discussed a data center moratorium. Second, we are monitoring 
where the hyperscalers themselves trade from a debt 
perspective. Borrowing costs in the high yield market are 
effectively priced as a derivative of where the hyperscalers 
trade. And, third, we are looking for the recent volatility in 
existing names to stabilize, which will be key to regaining 
investor interest in the market.  
Ultimately, though, a supply slowdown in the high yield market 
may not necessarily be a negative development. The 
underperformance of high yield credits over the summer was 
largely driven by fears of endless supply. With rates now higher 
and permitting a persistent challenge, new supply has been 
muted. Importantly, this doesn’t mean a massive backlog is 
building up; if a developer loses a lease or can’t secure a permit, 
the project will likely be shelved. By permanently removing 
such capacity from the pipeline, this dynamic should provide 
some natural support to the market over the medium-to-longer 
term. All of the existing supply is more valuable.
